If they don’t, I feel like I’m not managing myself very well, and that does affect what I feel comfortable eating. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit or used laxatives or diuretics after eating to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t made myself vomit or used those sorts of things after eating. I try to manage with what I choose to eat in the first place, if that makes sense. Is that alright to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently, or do you consider your weight to be much lower than normal for your height and build?\n\nPatient: It has gone down some, yes. My doctor seems more concerned about it than I am, honestly. I don’t think of it as terribly dramatic, but… when I look at those height and weight charts, I do still catch myself thinking that a little lower would feel like a neater, more manageable number. I know that may not be the right way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not most days. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t think of it as terribly dramatic, but… when I look at those height and weight charts, I do still catch myself thinking that a little lower would feel like a neater, more manageable number. I know that may not be the right way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not most days. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the enjoyment you get from activities you used to like has noticeably decreased compared to before?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s noticeably decreased, no. Maybe some days I’m a little less excited ahead of time, but once I’m doing something I usually can still enjoy it. I don’t think it’s a big change, if that’s what you mean. Does that sound like the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your appetite or found that you're less interested in eating, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it appetite exactly. I came in because my doctor was concerned that my meal planning has gotten a bit too tight. To me it just feels orderly—like yogurt for breakfast, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, then have a measured dinner so the day stays on track. I suppose that could look like being less interested in eating, but I don’t mean for it to be a problem. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt truly happy or enjoyed something? What was that like for you?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… yes, I mean, I can think of little things. Like if I’m with someone I like, or if something goes well at work, I do feel pleased. It’s not as if I don’t feel happiness.\n\nMaybe I don’t have one big example that stands out right this second, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things. Could you please tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my doctor seemed a bit concerned and thought I should talk to someone. I guess my meal planning has gotten… maybe a little strict, but I don’t really see it as a big problem. It just helps me feel orderly, you know? Usually I keep breakfast to yogurt, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, and then I make a very measured dinner. It feels sensible to me, but… I’m here because they thought it was worth discussing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself particularly concerned about your weight or the shape of your body, maybe to the point where it influences what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m trying to look young or anything like that… I know my age. But yes, I suppose I do pay attention to whether my waist and hips look, well, contained in my clothes. If they don’t, I feel like I’m not managing myself very well, and that does affect what I feel comfortable eating. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit or used laxatives or diuretics after eating to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t made myself vomit or used those sorts of things after eating. I try to manage with what I choose to eat in the first place, if that makes sense. Is that alright to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently, or do you consider your weight to be much lower than normal for your height and build?\n\nPatient: It has gone down some, yes. My doctor seems more concerned about it than I am, honestly. I don’t think of it as terribly dramatic, but… when I look at those height and weight charts, I do still catch myself thinking that a little lower would feel like a neater, more manageable number. I know that may not be the right way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not most days. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the enjoyment you get from activities you used to like has noticeably decreased compared to before?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s noticeably decreased, no. Maybe some days I’m a little less excited ahead of time, but once I’m doing something I usually can still enjoy it. I don’t think it’s a big change, if that’s what you mean. Does that sound like the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your appetite or found that you're less interested in eating, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it appetite exactly. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt truly happy or enjoyed something? Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or feel more tired than you used to, even if you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a major change. Usually I keep breakfast to yogurt, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, and then I make a very measured dinner. It feels sensible to me, but… I’m here because they thought it was worth discussing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself particularly concerned about your weight or the shape of your body, maybe to the point where it influences what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m trying to look young or anything like that… I know my age. But yes, I suppose I do pay attention to whether my waist and hips look, well, contained in my clothes. If they don’t, I feel like I’m not managing myself very well, and that does affect what I feel comfortable eating. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit or used laxatives or diuretics after eating to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I haven’t made myself vomit or used those sorts of things after eating. I try to manage with what I choose to eat in the first place, if that makes sense. Is that alright to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently, or do you consider your weight to be much lower than normal for your height and build?\n\nPatient: It has gone down some, yes. My doctor seems more concerned about it than I am, honestly. I don’t think of it as terribly dramatic, but… when I look at those height and weight charts, I do still catch myself thinking that a little lower would feel like a neater, more manageable number. I know that may not be the right way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not most days. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the enjoyment you get from activities you used to like has noticeably decreased compared to before?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s noticeably decreased, no. Maybe some days I’m a little less excited ahead of time, but once I’m doing something I usually can still enjoy it. I don’t think it’s a big change, if that’s what you mean. Does that sound like the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your appetite or found that you're less interested in eating, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it appetite exactly. I came in because my doctor was concerned that my meal planning has gotten a bit too tight. To me it just feels orderly—like yogurt for breakfast, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, then have a measured dinner so the day stays on track. I suppose that could look like being less interested in eating, but I don’t mean for it to be a problem. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt truly happy or enjoyed something? What was that like for you?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… yes, I mean, I can think of little things. Like if I’m with someone I like, or if something goes well at work, I do feel pleased. It’s not as if I don’t feel happiness.\n\nMaybe I don’t have one big example that stands out right this second, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have ordinary low moments, like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed or hopeless most days. I’ve been trying to keep things normal and manageable. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or feel more tired than you used to, even if you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a major change. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but that seems fairly normal to me—work, errands, just the usual day-to-day things. I don’t feel like I’m exhausted for no reason, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoes that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take less interest in things you used to care about, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I still do the things I usually care about, and I still want to keep up with them. Maybe some days I’m a bit less enthusiastic, but that feels normal to me.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest, if that’s what you’re asking. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how much weight you've lost recently, if any, and how that compares to what you consider a normal weight for your height?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s gone down some. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still functioning and everything, but my doctor has said it’s a bit lower than they’d like for my height.\n\nWhen I look at the height-and-weight chart, though, I have to admit I sort of feel like… a little lower would be a cleaner number, more manageable somehow. I know that may not be the “right” way to look at it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Could you please tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my doctor seemed a bit concerned and thought I should talk to someone. I guess my meal planning has gotten… maybe a little strict, but I don’t really see it as a big problem. It just helps me feel orderly, you know? Usually I keep breakfast to yogurt, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, and then I make a very measured dinner. It feels sensible to me, but… I’m here because they thought it was worth discussing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself particularly concerned about your weight or the shape of your body, maybe to the point where it influences what or how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m trying to look young or anything like that… I know my age. But yes, I suppose I do pay attention to whether my waist and hips look, well, contained in my clothes. If they don’t, I feel like I’m not managing myself very well, and that does affect what I feel comfortable eating. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit or used laxatives or diuretics after eating to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. My doctor seems more concerned about it than I am, honestly. I don’t think of it as terribly dramatic, but… when I look at those height and weight charts, I do still catch myself thinking that a little lower would feel like a neater, more manageable number. I know that may not be the right way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not most days. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the enjoyment you get from activities you used to like has noticeably decreased compared to before?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s noticeably decreased, no. Maybe some days I’m a little less excited ahead of time, but once I’m doing something I usually can still enjoy it. I don’t think it’s a big change, if that’s what you mean. Does that sound like the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your appetite or found that you're less interested in eating, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it appetite exactly. I came in because my doctor was concerned that my meal planning has gotten a bit too tight. To me it just feels orderly—like yogurt for breakfast, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, then have a measured dinner so the day stays on track. I suppose that could look like being less interested in eating, but I don’t mean for it to be a problem. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt truly happy or enjoyed something? What was that like for you?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… yes, I mean, I can think of little things. Like if I’m with someone I like, or if something goes well at work, I do feel pleased. It’s not as if I don’t feel happiness.\n\nMaybe I don’t have one big example that stands out right this second, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have ordinary low moments, like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed or hopeless most days. I’ve been trying to keep things normal and manageable. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or feel more tired than you used to, even if you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a major change. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but that seems fairly normal to me—work, errands, just the usual day-to-day things. I don’t feel like I’m exhausted for no reason, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoes that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take less interest in things you used to care about, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I still do the things I usually care about, and I still want to keep up with them. Maybe some days I’m a bit less enthusiastic, but that feels normal to me.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest, if that’s what you’re asking. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how much weight you've lost recently, if any, and how that compares to what you consider a normal weight for your height?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s gone down some. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still functioning and everything, but my doctor has said it’s a bit lower than they’d like for my height.\n\nWhen I look at the height-and-weight chart, though, I have to admit I sort of feel like… a little lower would be a cleaner number, more manageable somehow. I know that may not be the “right” way to look at it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, overall, you’re feeling down or less interested in life than you used to, even if it’s not all the time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have normal low days like anyone, but overall I wouldn’t say I’m feeling down about life or that I’ve stopped caring about things. I still keep up with what I need to and the things that matter to me.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your sense of how you are doing, or how you feel about yourself, depends a lot on how your weight or body shape seems to you?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, somewhat. I don’t like to make it sound vain, especially at my age—I’m not trying to look young or anything like that.\n\nBut I do notice I feel like I’m doing better, more in control maybe, if my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. If things feel tighter or less neat, I take that as a sign I’m not handling myself as well. I know that may not be the healthiest way to measure it, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current weight and how tall you are, so I can get a sense of where you fall on the weight-for-height spectrum?\n\nPatient: I’d rather not give the exact number if that’s okay… or, I mean, I can if you really need it. I’m average height, and my weight has just drifted down more than my doctor seems comfortable with.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to be difficult. When I look at the chart, I can see it’s lower than what they’d prefer for my height. But part of me still thinks if it were just a little lower, it would feel like a cleaner, more manageable number. I know that probably sounds odd.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My doctor seems more concerned about it than I am, honestly. I mean, I have quieter days, and I’m not always as enthusiastic about things as I probably used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I still manage what I need to do. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, or that you’ve lost interest in doing things that you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. I still do things if I’ve planned them, and I can enjoy them once I’m there. Maybe I’m a bit less… eager than I used to be, but I don’t know that it’s a real loss of pleasure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the enjoyment you get from activities you used to like has noticeably decreased compared to before?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s noticeably decreased, no. Maybe some days I’m a little less excited ahead of time, but once I’m doing something I usually can still enjoy it. I don’t think it’s a big change, if that’s what you mean. Does that sound like the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your appetite or found that you're less interested in eating, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it appetite exactly. I came in because my doctor was concerned that my meal planning has gotten a bit too tight. To me it just feels orderly—like yogurt for breakfast, and if I’m busy I might skip lunch, then have a measured dinner so the day stays on track. I suppose that could look like being less interested in eating, but I don’t mean for it to be a problem. Is that answering what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt truly happy or enjoyed something? What was that like for you?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… yes, I mean, I can think of little things. Like if I’m with someone I like, or if something goes well at work, I do feel pleased. It’s not as if I don’t feel happiness.\n\nMaybe I don’t have one big example that stands out right this second, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most days?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have ordinary low moments, like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed or hopeless most days. I’ve been trying to keep things normal and manageable. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or feel more tired than you used to, even if you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a major change. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but that seems fairly normal to me—work, errands, just the usual day-to-day things. I don’t feel like I’m exhausted for no reason, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoes that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you take less interest in things you used to care about, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I still do the things I usually care about, and I still want to keep up with them. Maybe some days I’m a bit less enthusiastic, but that feels normal to me.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest, if that’s what you’re asking. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how much weight you've lost recently, if any, and how that compares to what you consider a normal weight for your height?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s gone down some. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still functioning and everything, but my doctor has said it’s a bit lower than they’d like for my height.\n\nWhen I look at the height-and-weight chart, though, I have to admit I sort of feel like… a little lower would be a cleaner number, more manageable somehow. I know that may not be the “right” way to look at it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that, overall, you’re feeling down or less interested in life than you used to, even if it’s not all the time?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have normal low days like anyone, but overall I wouldn’t say I’m feeling down about life or that I’ve stopped caring about things. I still keep up with what I need to and the things that matter to me.\n\nI hope that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your sense of how you are doing, or how you feel about yourself, depends a lot on how your weight or body shape seems to you?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, somewhat. I don’t like to make it sound vain, especially at my age—I’m not trying to look young or anything like that.\n\nBut I do notice I feel like I’m doing better, more in control maybe, if my waist and hips look contained in my clothes. If things feel tighter or less neat, I take that as a sign I’m not handling myself as well. I know that may not be the healthiest way to measure it, but that’s sort of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current weight and how tall you are, so I can get a sense of where you fall on the weight-for-height spectrum?\n\nPatient: I’d rather not give the exact number if that’s okay… or, I mean, I can if you really need it. I’m average height, and my weight has just drifted down more than my doctor seems comfortable with.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to be difficult. When I look at the chart, I can see it’s lower than what they’d prefer for my height. But part of me still thinks if it were just a little lower, it would feel like a cleaner, more manageable number. I know that probably sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you feel down or less interested in things nearly every day for most of the day, and if so, how long have you felt that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I have days where I’m a bit flat or tired of things, but not nearly every day for most of the day.\n\nAt least, I haven’t noticed a stretch like that. If I’m understanding you correctly, I wouldn’t say it’s been going on in that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
